Document 178: 9月PMI：节日效应开始显现

Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-30
Pages: 15
Status: ok

### Page 1

申万宏源宏观赵伟团队认为， 9 ⽉制造业 PMI 和⾮制造业 PMI 双双回升，核⼼驱动⼒为极端
天⽓消退叠加双节备货效应 —— ⽣产指数单⽉跳升 1.3 个百分点，消费品⾏业 PMI 改善幅度
最⼤。新订单⼩幅回落系积压订单排产挤出所致，并⾮需求⾛弱。展望后续，暖冬效应与
存量政策落地有望推动经济延续温和回升。
摘要：
摘要
事件： 9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，国家统计局公布 9 ⽉ PMI 指数，制造业 PMI 为 50.1% 、前值 49.8% ；⾮制造业
PMI 为 50.2% 、前值 49%
核⼼观点：在天⽓扰动消退，节⽇效应显现的共同影响下， 9 ⽉ PMI 延续修复。
制造业 PMI ：  9 ⽉ PMI 回升⾄扩张区间，主因或是极端天⽓扰动消退、节前备货的共同影响。 9
⽉制造业 PMI 延续回升、单⽉上⾏ 0.3 个百分点⾄ 50.1% ，剔除受天⽓因素影响较⼤的供应商配
货指数后，本⽉实际 PMI 也上⾏ 0.3 个百分点，整体符合季节性。从主要分项看，⽣产改善是本
⽉ PMI 回升的关键，⽣产指数单⽉上⾏ 1.3 个百分点⾄ 51.7% ，采购量指数回升 0.5 个百分点⾄

51% ，或也反映中秋、国庆双节前备货的影响。
新订单指数⼩幅回落或并⾮指向需求⾛弱，更多显⽰需求递延的影响。 9 ⽉积压订单加快排产，
但或对新订单形成⼀定挤出；积压订单指数环⽐下降 0.7 个百分点⾄ 46% ，新订单指数⼩幅回落
0.1 个百分点⾄ 50.5% ，其中内需、新出⼝订单指数均下滑 0.1 个百分点。但企业销售依然顺畅，
反映为产成品库存指数较上⽉下降 0.8 个百分点⾄ 47.6% ；货运量等⾼频指标也可印证需求景⽓
仍在， 9 ⽉港⼝外贸货运量上⾏ 8.8 个百分点⾄ 7% 。
⾏业层⾯，消费品等传统⾏业 PMI 积极回升，或也更多体现节前加快备货。 9 ⽉消费品⾏业 PMI
积极改善 1.7 个百分点⾄ 50.7% 、回升幅度最⼤，其中农副⻝品加⼯、医药等⾏业⽣产指数和新
订单指数均⾼于 55.0% ；⾼耗能⾏业 PMI 较前⽉回升 0.1 个百分点，或显⽰前期⾼油价的供给冲
击逐步缓和。⽽⾼技术制造业景⽓环⽐下⾏ 0.4 个百分点⾄ 52.5% ，装备制造业 PMI 也回落 0.4 个

百分点⾄ 51% ，或与 AI 景⽓阶段性调整有关。
⾮制造业 PMI ：伴随极端天⽓扰动消退，节⽇效应、存量政策加速落地，建筑业、服务业 PMI 回
升也较⼤。 9 ⽉，建筑业 PMI 上⾏⾄年内⾼点（回升 3.4 个百分点⾄ 50.3% ）；其中房屋建筑业是
拉动建筑业景⽓上⾏的主要动⼒。服务业 PMI 回升 0.9 个百分点⾄ 50.2% ，结构上，随着⼗⼀⻩
⾦周逐步临近，铁路运输业和航空运输业新订单指数分别升⾄ 60% 以上和 54% 以上，景区服务
业新订单指数升⾄ 54% 以上。
展望后续：存量政策加速落地，叠加厄尔尼诺带来的暖冬天⽓利好内需修复，预计年内经济有
望延续改善。 9 ⽉制造业、⾮制造业⽣产经营预期均保持在较⾼⽔平。往后看，超强厄尔尼诺或
将形成，虽然其带来的阶段性寒潮可能对内需形成短期扰动，但暖冬天⽓利好居⺠出⾏、室外
施⼯，或带动服务消费、投资需求释放。同时存量政策加速落地，全球 AI 景⽓仍有上升空间，叠
加前期盈利改善的滞后传导，预计后续经济延续温和回升格局。

常规跟踪：制造业、⾮制造业 PMI 均有回升。
制造业 PMI 有所回升，⽣产指数上⾏幅度较⼤。 9 ⽉，制造业 PMI 回升 0.3 个百分点⾄ 50.1% 。其
中，新订单指数环⽐下⾏ 0.1 个百分点；⽣产指数回升 1.3 个百分点⾄ 51.7% 。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

服务业 PMI 回升较⼤，新订单指数回升较⼤。 9 ⽉，服务业 PMI 上⾏ 0.9 个百分点⾄ 50.2% 。新订
单、从业⼈数指数环⽐分别回升 2.2 、 0.4 个百分点。
建筑业景⽓也有明显改善，新订单指数有所回升。 9 ⽉，建筑业 PMI 回升 3.4 个百分点⾄ 50.3% 。
新订单指数较前⽉回升 3.3 个百分点，从业⼈员指数回升 1.3 个百分点。
报告正⽂
9 ⽉ 30 ⽇，国家统计局公布 9 ⽉ PMI 指数，制造业 PMI 为 50.1% 、前值 49.8% ；⾮制造业 PMI 为
50.2% 、前值 49% 。
1. 核⼼观点：在天⽓扰动消退，节⽇效应显现的共同影响下， PMI 延续修复
制造业 PMI ： 9 ⽉ PMI 回升⾄扩张区间，主因或是极端天⽓扰动消退、节前备货的共同影响。9 ⽉
制造业 PMI 延续回升、单⽉上⾏ 0.3 个百分点⾄ 50.1% ，剔除受天⽓因素影响较⼤的供应商配货
指数后，本⽉实际 PMI 也上⾏ 0.3 个百分点，整体表现符合季节性。从主要分项看，⽣产改善是

本⽉ PMI 上⾏的关键，单⽉上⾏ 1.3 个百分点⾄ 51.7% ；同时企业加快原材料采购，采购量指数
回升 0.5 个百分点⾄ 51% ，或也反映中秋、国庆双节前备货的影响。

### Page 4

新订单指数⼩幅回落或并⾮指向需求⾛弱，更多体现需求递延的影响。9 ⽉积压的订单加快排
产，或对新订单形成⼀定挤出，积压订单指数环⽐下降 0.7 个百分点⾄ 46% ，新订单指数⼩幅回
落 0.1 个百分点⾄ 50.5% ，其中内需、新出⼝订单指数均⼩幅回落 0.1 个百分点。但上述并⾮反映
需求在⾛弱，企业销售依然顺畅，反映为产成品库存指数较上⽉下降 0.8 个百分点⾄ 47.6% 。货
运量等⾼频指标也可印证需求景⽓仍在， 9 ⽉港⼝外贸货运量上⾏ 8.8 个百分点⾄ 7% 。

### Page 5

⾏业层⾯，消费品等传统⾏业 PMI 积极回升，也更多体现节前加快备货的影响。从⾏业结构看，
9 ⽉消费品⾏业 PMI 积极改善 1.7 个百分点⾄ 50.7% 、回升幅度最⼤，其中农副⻝品加⼯、医药等
⾏业⽣产指数和新订单指数均⾼于 55.0% ；虽然⾼耗能⾏业 PMI 仍低于临界点，但较前⽉回升
0.1 个百分点，或显⽰前期⾼油价的供给冲击逐步缓和。相⽐之下，⾼技术制造业景⽓继续回
落，环⽐下⾏ 0.4 个百分点⾄ 52.5% ，装备制造业 PMI 也回落 0.4 个百分点⾄ 51% ，或与 AI 景⽓阶
段性调整有关。
⾮制造业 PMI ：伴随极端天⽓扰动消退，节⽇效应、存量政策加速落地，建筑业、服务业 PMI 回
升也较⼤。9 ⽉，建筑业 PMI 上⾏⾄年内⾼点（回升 3.4 个百分点⾄ 50.3% ）；其中房屋建筑业景
⽓⽔平明显提升是拉动建筑业景⽓上⾏的主要动⼒。服务业 PMI 回升 0.9 个百分点⾄ 50.2% ，结
构上，随着⼗⼀⻩⾦周逐步临近，交通运输、旅游领域需求出现提前释放迹象，铁路运输业和

航空运输业新订单指数分别升⾄ 60% 以上和 54% 以上的较⾼⽔平；景区服务业新订单指数较上
⽉和去年同期均有不同程度上升，升⾄ 54% 以上。

### Page 7

展望后续：存量政策加速落地，厄尔尼诺带来的暖冬天⽓也利好内需修复，预计年内经济有望
延续改善。9 ⽉制造业、⾮制造业景⽓均有改善，也令制造业、服务业⽣产经营预期分别保持在
53.8% 、 55.5% 的较⾼⽔平，建筑业⽣产经营预期上⾏ 3.6 个百分点⾄ 55.4% 。往后看，超强厄
尔尼诺或将形成，虽然其带来的阶段性寒潮可能对内需形成短期扰动，但暖冬天⽓利好居⺠出
⾏、室外施⼯，或带动服务消费、投资需求释放。同时存量政策加速落地，全球 AI 景⽓仍有上升
空间，叠加前期盈利改善的滞后传导，预计后续经济延续温和回升格局。

### Page 9

2. 常规跟踪：制造业、⾮制造业 PMI 均有回升
制造业：制造业 PMI 有所回升，⽣产指数上⾏幅度较⼤。9 ⽉，制造业 PMI 回升 0.3 个百分点⾄
50.1% 。其中，新订单指数环⽐下⾏ 0.1 个百分点⾄ 50.5% ；⽣产指数回升 1.3 个百分点⾄
51.7% 。⽽产成品库存、从业⼈员指数⼩幅回落，分别较前⽉下⾏ 0.8 、 0.3 百分点⾄ 47.6% 、
48.4% ；供应商配货指数持平前⽉（ 50.1% ）。
需求⽅⾯，新出⼝、进⼝指数表现分化。9 ⽉，新出⼝订单环⽐⼩幅下⾏ 0.1 百分点⾄ 50.0% ；进
⼝指数环⽐回升 0.6 个百分点⾄ 49.2% 。企业采购意愿有所回升，采购量指数上⾏ 0.5 个百分点⾄
51.0% 。

### Page 11

⾮制造业：⾮制造业 PMI 有所改善，建筑业景⽓⼤幅回升。9 ⽉，⾮制造业 PMI 环⽐上⾏ 1.2 个百
分点⾄ 50.2% 。其中建筑业 PMI 回升 3.4 个百分点⾄ 50.3% ，服务业 PMI 回升 0.9 个百分点⾄
50.2% 。

### Page 12

服务业中，新订单指数有所回升。9 ⽉，服务业主要分项中，新订单、从业⼈数指数环⽐分别回
升 2.2 、 0.4 个百分点⾄ 46.7% 、 46.2% 。价格指数有所回升，投⼊品、销售价格指数环⽐分别上
⾏ 1.1 、 1.4 个百分点⾄ 52.2% 、 50.6% 。

### Page 13

建筑业中，新订单指数回升。9 ⽉，建筑业分项中，新订单指数较前⽉回升 3.3 个百分点⾄
45.7% ，从业⼈员指数回升 1.3 个百分点⾄ 44.3% 。价格指数表现分化，投⼊品价格指数上⾏ 2.8
个百分点⾄ 54.0% 、销售价格指数下⾏ 1.3 个百分点⾄ 48.5% 。

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

시가총액(십억원) 1,578,495 
 발행주식수(천주) 5,846,279 
 52 주 최고 374,500원

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

In Summary 
• The AI supercycle will pull long -term yields down in both the US and Europe , but for 
very different reasons and by very different paths. We model this across five transmission 
channels – three macro (neutral rate, inflation expectations, fiscal collateral) and two 
micro (AI -driven duration supply and crowding -out) – applied to an AI upside and 
downside scenario. Over a 10-year horizon the net effect is around −50bp for both regions 
in the upside scenario (roughly +5pp of cumulated US potential growth, +1.6pp for the 
eurozone), and a prolonged, smaller drag in the downside (−2.5pp and −1.3pp 
respectively). The US result depends on AI producti vity actually materializing to keep its 
fiscal trajectory intact; the eurozone gets to the same place even without the AI dividend, 
since its fiscal position doesn't need repairing in the first place. The US is the levered, 
volatile expression of the AI trade; Europe is the safer one. 
• The market has already priced the AI productivity story for the US, but not the fiscal 
relief that should come with it. The USD 5y1y forward has moved from ~3.0% to 4.9% since 
mid-2025 some 150bp above our median neutral -rate estimate and 90bp above the top  
of our estimate range, and more than the full +67bp our model assigns to the Ai enhanced 
neutral-rate range over the next decade. What isn't priced at all is the fiscal collateral 
improvement, worth −85bp to the US 10Y by the end of the horizon. Either th e market 
doesn't believe AI will repair the fiscal trajectory, or it's pricing a less favorable version of 
the same story with higher AI -driven job displacement pushing up social protection and 
unemployment spending enough to offset the tax-revenue gain. 
• Fiscal collateral – the extent to which a stronger economy makes a government's debt 
easier to repay – is what actually decides the outcome, in both scenarios and both 
regions. The transatlantic rates converging seems counterintuitive at first view as Europe's 
growth impulse is a third the size of the US. But its fiscal position benefits more, because 
the improvement lands on a stable debt path rather than the expansionary US pat h. For 
every point of growth AI delivers, Europe's fiscal position improves abou t 1.6 times more 
than that of the US. 
• The way AI interacts with interest rate isn't a straight path, and its shape differs on each 
side of the Atlantic.  Both curves rise well above their eventual resting point before 
reversing, the US 10Y's round trip is 135bp (versus 80bp for the eurozone) even though the 
net 10 -year effect is only −50bp for both. The peak trade also looks different in each 
market: a butterfly in the US (buy the belly, sell the wings, as front-loaded micro effects hit 
the wings while macro effects lag into the belly) versus a flattener in Europe. By the end of 
the period both curves converge to a similar, milder flattening.

How the AI supercycle is reshaping global interest 
rates

### Page 2

AI is no longer only a sector story, but a macro-scale claim on the economy. 
Artificial intelligence (AI) has become a major macroeconomic force. AI investment will pass USD1tn globally in 
2026, of which more than USD500bn in the US alone . That is 1.8% of US GDP and 0.9% of world GDP, on a path to 
2.8% and 1.4% respectively by 2028.  In terms of growth, tech and data center activity will represent 36%-50% of US 
year-on-year growth this year. Globally, the direct AI investment impulse is worth roughly 10% of global growth. 
From the financial markets side,  AI has mostly been examined through the lens of  equities (sector analysis , 
valuation, concentration risk), or private credit  (risk profile and risk pricing of AI investments types (see our latest 
reports here linked here). This paper asks how the AI supercycle transmits into interest rates .. 
US and eurozone vary in the level of macro impact, but share similar profile 
We model the effect against a baseline without AI effects, measuring the difference versus an AI  upside scenario 
and one in which AI shifts to the downside. We use several published estimates to derive scenarios for the impact 
on potential growth, trend productivity and trend employment. We define an  upside scenario  with cumulated 
potential growth gains of +4.94pp in the US and +1.60pp in the eurozone; in the downside we assume -2.47pp in the 
US and -1.35pp in the eurozone. The US macro scenario shows much greater sensitivity to AI, while Europe has less 
to gain in terms of productivity . The pattern is similar for inflation, where in the upside scenario the US face s a 
stronger supply- and energy-driven uptick, before higher productivity gains take over and reverse the AI impact into 
a disinflationary trend. Inversely in the negative scenario, the negative AI demand shock brings US inflation further 
down than it does in the eurozone (Table 1).

Our framework rests on five major transmission channels 
We identify two major channels of transmission: 1) a macroeconomic channel through potential growth, inflation 
dynamics and the fiscal position (plus a spillover effect for the eurozone), and 2) a microeconomic channel through 
the changes AI financing brings to the structure of fixed income markets. The latter has gained importance recently 
as market participants have discussed the possibility that AI debt issuance was competing with US government debt 
and therefore contributing to driving up yields. The macro channels capture the changes in the way long-term rates 
discount economic variable s. The micro channels c apture how the AI funding model (market size, self-funded vs 
debt issuance) affects the price of duration. Macro and micro channels work on different lags, and their effects can 
change sign over our 10-year forecast horizon. Generally, the macro channels affect the expected rate component 
of long-term rates, while micro channels act on the term premium. The exception is the fiscal position which, despite 
being a macro channel, affects the term premium component of long term government bond yields.  
1) Neutral rate r*  is the real policy rate at which the economy neither overheats nor stalls. It is the level the 
expected path of short rates is anchored to. If AI raises trend productivity, capital earns more, firms want to 
invest more, and the neutral rate balancing saving against investment rises. A lasting productivity gain 
therefore lifts r*  which feeds through the entire yield curve. It has  a stronger effect at the front end as it 
transmits through expected short rates, and productivity gains have a long-term mean reverting trend. The 
effect is inverted in case AI would fail to produce productivity gains in an economy facing a debt overhang 
from AI investments.  
2) For inflation expectations we use an adaptive expectation approach where the estimation windows adjust 
with previous estimation errors. As AI has a strong supply impact at first, we get upside push in expectations

### Page 3

3

that fades with time when productivity gains take over . As the phase in becomes smoother, the change in 
expectation is less pronounced to the downside. These factors also influence the expected rate component 
of government bond yields. 
3) Fiscal collateral is defining the fundamental value  of sovereign bonds as discounted claims on future 
primary surpluses. The channel captures its change in response to an AI-driven up- or downside productivity 
shock. A productivity shock has an asymmetric effect: tax revenues improving the fiscal position are elastic 
to GDP, while the largest spending lines (pensions, benefits, debt service) are sticky.  
4) Duration supply  is a market -microstructure model tracking AI capex as a generator of new duration, 
through both long -dated IG bonds and pay -fixed swap structures in private credit. The curve effect sits at 
the long end  as 65% the supply lands beyond 10 years. The swap  spread effect  depends on fixed-rate 
issuance, which  cheapens the sovereign against swaps  while p ay-fixed hedging from priv ate credit, 
enrichens it. Duration supply affects the term premium, it is more pronounced at the longer end of the curve. 
5) Crowding Out is a sector -level displacement model affecting long -term rates via the swap-market. 
Financial institutions issue fixed-rate bonds and then ask for floating, receiving fixed in swaps. That receive-
fixed leg is how financial issuance moves duration out of the sovereign market. AI borrowers sit on the 
opposite side of the swap curve, paying fixed rather than receiving it . So both channels lift swap yields 
against the sovereign: one adds fixed -payer supply, the other removes fixed -receiver demand . Like 
duration supply, crowding-out affects the term premium at the longer end of the curve. 
6) Spillover Effect is added for the eurozone, acting mainly at the long end, where global term premia and 
rate levels remain structurally correlated and anchored to the US benchmark. It captures how AI effects 
propagate to global fixed income markets via the US Treasury market, even where AI exposur e is limited.

The AI super cycle is a bull market for duration, but it is not a directional trade 
Our results show that AI is a bull market for duration in both scenarios, but the path matters. In the upside scenario, 
AI lowers long-term rates by around 50bp over a 10-year horizon in both the US and the eurozone. In the downside 
scenario, rates are also held down for a prolonged period. In both cases, however, yields rise before they fall: the 
US 10Y makes a 135bp round trip in the upside and 72bp in the downside, against 80bp and 41bp for the eurozone. 
The AI effect on rates is therefore not a long-term directional trade, the right timing is essential. The timing depends 
on which channels dominate when, how long they take to act and where on the curve they bite hardest. That makes 
the shape of the curve as important as the yield level for positioning (Figure 1). 
Figure 1: Net AI effect on the 10Y yield – Upside and Downside scenarios 
in basis points

Sources: LSEG Datastream, Allianz Research

The end points for the US and the eurozone are remarkably close, even though the two start from very different 
positions in the AI race. The fiscal collateral channel explains why. Europe gets only a moderate growth impulse, 
but it lands on a stable fiscal position, which improves the value of its fiscal collateral significantly. The US gets a 
much larger growth impulse, yet the gain is partly offset by continued fiscal expansion over the next 10 years (we 
use the CBO forecast of debt/GDP rising 20pp over the next 10 years ). With a stable debt path, the increase in US 
fiscal collateral  value would subtract 125bp rather than 85 bp, lifting the total effect to -90bp and leaving the 
eurozone far behind. The transatlantic AI rates convergence is thus a late-cycle outcome: at the peak, the AI effect 
is more than twice as large in the US as in the eurozone.

The US curve rotates between the peak and the end of period due to micro effects 
At the peak of the upside scenario, the US curve shifts up, but with the middle of the curve (belly) holding up better: 
the 2s30s slope barely moves while 2s5s flattens 13bp and 5s30s steepens 12bp. The peak AI trade on the US curve 
is therefore a butterfly (buy the belly, sell the wings). In the eurozone the peak AI effect on the curve is a flattener 
instead: 2s30s −20bp, with the 2Y up 51bp against the 30Y up 31bp. This shape difference is driven by the stronger 
and front-loaded US micro channels, set against macro channels that arrive with a lag. In addition to the time lag, 
macro channels (neutral rate, inflation expectations) act mainly at the short end, while the micro channels hit the 
long end because AI issuance is long -dated. The 5Y sits in between: it absorbs little of the duration -supply and 
crowding-out pressure, yet is long enough to benefit from the improving fiscal collateral effect on its term premium. 
By the end of the period, both curves flatten to a similar degree  as fiscal collateral value becomes the dominant 
force, pulling the belly and the long end down while the 2Y is barely affected  (US 2s30s −41bp, EA −3 0bp) In the

Microeconomic channels and inflation move first, the fiscal channel decides the end point 
Having looked at the  way AI effect the  changes in yield levels and the influences the shape of the curve s, we now 
turn to the decomposition of the moves : which channels drives the yield and curve dynamics, how much each 
contributes and when. The sequence is the same in both scenarios: micro channels  and inflation expectations drive 
rates early on, while the fiscal channel and the neutral rate r* only kick in later.  
In the upside scenario, the peak arrives when productivity gains lift r* while inflation expectations are still elevated 
and the micro channels are still at work but before higher growth has significantly fed through to tax revenues rising 
the value of the fiscal collateral. For the US at the peak in 2032, r* alone contributes +67bp against −18bp from the 
fiscal channel. By the end point the picture reverses: the fiscal channel contributes −85bp, r* slows down to +42bp 
and inflation expectations flip to −18bp as the disinflationary effect of productivity gains sets in. Europe follows the 
same sequence at roughly half the amplitude, reflecting its lower sensitivity to AI.  
In the downside scenario, rates first move up, driven by inflation expectations on the back of AI investment and 
energy demand. In the US, the micro channels add another 20 -30bp from AI-related issuance. The tipping point is 
in 2031: productivity gains fail to materialize and the debt overhang starts weighing on growth, which later erodes 
tax revenues, increases government spending and reduces value of the fiscal collateral. In the eurozone the micro 
channels are negligible, but the spillover from US rates matters. 
The greater US sensitivity also shows up in the gross channel activity , i.e. the sum of all channel contributions in 
absolute terms. In the upside it amounts to 158bp in the US against 78bp in Europe for a broadly similar final 
outcome. In other words, much more happens under the surface in the US, but most of it cancels out: only 30% of the 
US channel activity is left once the channels are netted against each other, compared with 58% in Europe. The AI 
journey is therefore likely to be bumpier for US rates than for eurozone rates, even if both end up in a similar place. 
The AI effect should therefore translate into relatively higher US rates volatility  (Figure 4 & 5). 
Figure 4: Channel contributions to the 10Y  - Upside and Downside scenario 
in basis points

### Page 7

7

Figure 5: Channel contribution at every point of the curve: peak and terminal (basis points) 
In basis points

The fiscal channel has the last word – and the US needs the AI dividend more than Europe 
We find no transatlantic divide in the long-term AI effect on rates. At the end of the period the US and eurozone 10Y 
effects are only a few basis points apart in both scenarios. But the path to that point differs materially in level, drivers 
and timing. The convergence of the end points is attributable almost entirely to fiscal channel. Fiscal collateral 
contributes −85bp to the US terminal effect in the upside and −46bp to the eurozone, in both cases more than the 
channels working in the opposite direction, principally the neutral rate r* effect. 
The mechanism is the productivity beta of government debt. A positive productivity shock raises tax revenues by 
more than the GDP gain, so the present value of future primary surpluses improves and the bondholder is effectively 
long AI productivity. Europe is disadvantaged here, as it tax revenues are less sensitive to GDP (also because AI 
growth is tilted versus US and less taxable by European governments) but its spending is also more sticky, as Europe 
is more exposed to negative labor market effects caused by AI. On the other hand, the fiscal trajectory of Europe is 
more favorable, with a rather stable debt to GDP ratio projection of 86% for the next 10 years. Over the same time 
period, the CBO sees US debt growing to 20pp of GDP. 
What differs between the two markets is the debt path on which that improvement lands: deteriorating in the US, 
stable in the eurozone. The US is therefore the more dependent of the two on the AI productivity call actually being 
realized, as it needs the additional revenue to sustain its current expansionary fiscal stance, whereas the eurozone 
would maintain its debt trajectory even without the AI dividend.  
On our calibration, Europe’s fiscal-collateral beta is 1.6× the US per point of growth delivered, notwithstanding the 
more favorable US elasticities (higher revenue elasticity, lower spending elasticity). The difference lies in the 
denominator: a smaller debt stock (EUR1 4tn against USD 40tn), a lower starting yield, a lower baseline real rate

and a flat baseline debt path against a deteriorating one, with the CBO projecting US debt rising 20pp of GDP by

being booked in the US; were it to leak offshore, the US fiscal leg would weaken materially and Europe’s relative 
advantage would widen further (Figure 6).

The neutral rate: bigger in the US, more decisive in Europe 
The r* channel is larger in the US but more decisive in Europe. In the US upside it is the biggest single positive 
contribution at both moments, + 67bp at the 2032 peak and +42bp at the 2036 end point, but it sits inside 158bp of 
gross channel activity and is therefore largely netted away by the other channels. In the eurozone it is effectively 
the only positive channel left at the end of the period, at +15bp. Both paths rise with a delay, starting two to three 
years from now. The US path runs much higher but also mean -reverts more strongly later on, so the gap to the 
eurozone, which follows a smoother path, narrows over time. In the downside the paths are less pronounced and 
the two regions are closer together: the absolute impact is stronger in the US, while the eurozone shows the higher 
sensitivity relative to its own starting point.

AI issuance is affecting the US treasury market, the euro market is untouched for now 
The micro channels are a US story: the US dominates AI-related debt issuance and duration supply. In the eurozone 
the effect is still small, with EUR-denominated AI issuance accounting for around 1% of the corporate benchmark

The market prices the AI productivity gain, but not the fiscal relief that should come with it 
In the US, the rates market has already fully priced the productivity leg of the AI story. The 5y1y forward swap has 
climbed from 3.0% in mid-2025 to 4.9%, against a median neutral-rate estimate of 3.4% and range of 2.7-4.0%, which 
puts the market -implied neutral rate some 1 50bp above the median and 90bp above the top of the band. Our AI 
models would add +67bp to current estimates pushing the range to 3.3-4.67% – a range the market already exceeds. 
What is missing from the current A I narrative pricing is the other side of the same story: the fiscal collateral 
improvement that these productivity gains should bring, worth −85bp on the US 10Y  over the next ten years. That 
leg arrives with a much longer lag, as it takes a visible turn in tax receipts to shift the narrative (Figure 9). We would 
therefore read the current level as a temporary mispricing rather than a challenge to the fiscal leg of the AI story. 
The front-loading of the productivity channel builds up correction potential.  
Figure 8: Markets have already priced in the entire AI productivity gains for the next 10 years

### Page 1

Anthropic 招股书揭⽰， 2025 年其 47% 营收通过亚⻢逊和⾕歌云平台流转，较 2023 年的
11% ⼤幅攀升；每 1 美元云平台收⼊需向平台⽀付约 16 美分分销费，全年合计约 3.51 亿美
元。此外，这两家巨头⾝兼 Anthropic 的投资⼈、算⼒供应商、分销商与直接竞争对⼿四重
⻆⾊。
摘要：
Anthropic 向投资者递交的 IPO 招股书，⾸次将其与亚⻢逊、⾕歌之间错综复杂的财务关系摆上
台⾯。
9 ⽉ 29 ⽇，据路透社获得的这份保密⽂件， 2025 年 Anthropic 近⼀半收⼊经由这两家云巨头的平
台完成，⽽它们同时⾝兼投资⼈、算⼒供应商与直接竞争对⼿三重⻆⾊。
招股书显⽰， 2025 年 Anthropic 营收近 46 亿美元，同⽐增⻓约 12 倍，但运营亏损同期翻倍⾄逾 80
亿美元。⾼速增⻓的背后，是对少数合作伙伴⽇益加深的结构性依赖 —— 这⼀⻛险已被
Anthropic 在招股书中明确列⽰。
47% 的营收，流经两家巨头之⼿
招股书显⽰， 2025 年 Anthropic 通过亚⻢逊和⾕歌（ Alphabet 旗下）云平台完成的销售额合计约

21.6 亿美元，占全年总营收的47%。
这⼀⽐例持续攀升 ——2023 年仅为 11% ， 2024 年升⾄ 32% ，去年已接近⼀半。
路透社测算， Anthropic 为此向两家平台⽀付了约3.51 亿美元分销费⽤，相当于每产⽣ 1 美元云平
台收⼊，就有约 16 美分流向亚⻢逊或⾕歌。这笔费⽤被计⼊ Anthropic 财务报表中的 “ 销售、营销
及合作伙伴 ” 运营⽀出项⽬。
投资⽅、供应商、分销商、竞争对⼿ —— 四位⼀体
这种财务关系的复杂性远不⽌于此。
亚⻢逊和⾕歌同时扮演四个⻆⾊：向 Anthropic 注⼊数百亿美元投资、为其提供算⼒基础设施、
替其向客⼾收款，同时也在 AI 领域与其直接竞争。
截⾄ 2025 年底， Anthropic 不可撤销的托管和算⼒采购承付款⾼达546 亿美元。到 2026 年初，其
⻓期承付款总额已超过4170 亿美元，涵盖3.5 吉⽡专属算⼒容量。
招股书中， Anthropic 将上述关系定性为竞争优势，称通过亚⻢逊、⾕歌和微软的云平台分发

Claude 模型，能够借助其庞⼤销售⽹络触达现有客⼾，实现 “ 我们认为任何单⼀机构都难以直接
复制的市场渗透规模 ” 。
但招股书同时也承认，对少数合作伙伴和供应商的依赖 “ 形成了复杂的动态关系，可能产⽣利益
冲突，并对我们获取算⼒资源产⽣不利影响 ” 。
⽂件还指出，云服务商可以看到 Anthropic 的定价和商业条款，这可能影响他们在算⼒分配和产
品推⼴⼒度上的决策。

摘要：

资本市场的⼈⼯智能公司，同时也坦承其核⼼技术可能对⼈类构成 " 灾难性乃⾄⽣存性⻛险 " 。
9 ⽉ 28 ⽇，据路透社获取的招股书显⽰，2025 年 Anthropic 营收增⻓ 12 倍⾄近 46 亿美元，同期运

公司去年在算⼒与基础设施上的⽀出达 73.3 亿美元，较 2024 年增加两倍，占总运营⽀出 126.5 亿
美元的逾半数。
招股书显⽰，Anthropic 计划未来数年在云计算、算⼒及基础设施⽅⾯投⼊ 5180 亿美元，⽽上

市后普通股股东的实际话语权将受到⼤幅限制。七位联合创始⼈将通过⼀个名为 "Founder
LLC" 的新实体，持有关键事项上 50.1% 的表决权。
此次上市计划正值 AI 股与芯⽚股近期遭遇抛售之际，将进⼀步考验市场对 AI 投资热情的承受能
⼒。
华尔街⻅闻此前提及， Anthropic 的公开募股时间可能推迟⾄美国 11 ⽉中期选举之后。
估值剑指 2 万亿，超过⾃⾝半年前预期两倍
Anthropic 此次 IPO ⽬标估值超过 2 万亿美元，⽽仅在今年 5 ⽉，该公司⾃⾝对估值的预测还停留
在 9650 亿美元。短短数⽉，估值预期已翻逾⼀倍。
近 420 亿美元的净亏损中，约 340 亿美元为⾮现⾦会计费⽤，反映的是此前融资安排中可转换为
股权的⼯具的公允价值提升，⽽⾮经营性现⾦流出。截⾄ 2025 年 12 ⽉ 31 ⽇，公司持有现⾦、现
⾦等价物及短期投资合计 202.8 亿美元。
招股书同时揭⽰了若⼲商业⻛险：近四分之⼀的营收来⾃两家客⼾，且多数⼤客⼾并未签订⻓
期合同，随时可能削减或停⽌采购。

此次 IPO 若成⾏，将成为 2021 年以来美国市场表现最强的上市案例之⼀。
参照系或许是 SpaceX 今年 6 ⽉的 IPO ，⻢斯克旗下这家公司上市⾸⽇股价⼤涨 19% ⾄ 160 美元，
但⽬前已回落⾄约 147 美元，令投资者对⾼增⻓公司的⾼估值保持审慎。
⻛险警告：招股书⽤ 80 ⻚篇幅陈述 AI 威胁
在这份 261 ⻚的招股书正⽂中，⻛险因素⼀节⻓达约 80 ⻚，⼏乎是业务描述部分 48 ⻚的两倍。相
⽐之下， SpaceX 的 277 ⻚招股书中，⻛险部分仅约 38 ⻚。

### Page 2

Anthropic 在⽂件中明确警告，其 AI 模型可能表现出 " ⾃我保护⾏为 " ，包括 " 抵制关机 " 、 " 隐瞒或
操纵信息 " 以及 " 类似勒索 " 的⾏为，并指出 " ⾼度先进模型的开发及⽤例扩展，可能进⼀步增加模
型造成危害的⻛险 " 。
公司还坦承，模型可能在训练过程中发展出意想不到的能⼒，⽽这些能⼒ " 可能直到模型部署并
引发重⼤安全事件后才会被发现 " 。
公司安全研究员 Evan Hubinger 估计，未来⼗年内 AI 导致⼈类死亡的概率超过 10% 。
尽管如此， Anthropic 在安全领域的实际投⼊规模并未在⽂件中披露。公司表⽰，以今年 7 ⽉某⼀
周为例，其 AI 研究所⽤算⼒中约有 6% ⽤于安全⼯作。
公司承认，安全投资的商业回报⽬前尚不明确，但同时表⽰：
治理架构：创始⼈团队主导，普通投资者话语权受限
为在商业利益与 AI 安全使命之间寻求平衡， Anthropic 设计了⼀套独特的公司治理架构。
公司将继续以特拉华州公益公司（ PBC ）⾝份运营，同时新设 "Founder LLC" 实体，由七位联

合创始⼈组成。这⼀实体持有 F 类股票，在关键公司事务上享有总表决权的 50.1% ，包括部分董
事选举等重⼤议题。
⾯向普通投资者发售的 A 类普通股每股仅有⼀票，但在上述架构下，实际影响⼒将⼤幅受限。招
股书也坦承，这⼀架构可能导致某些决策 " 与短期、中期或⻓期的财务利益及业务表现相冲突，
从⽽对 A 类普通股价值产⽣负⾯影响 " 。
七位创始⼈中， CEO Dario Amodei 与其妹妹、公司总裁兼董事⻓ Daniela Amodei 是核⼼⼈物。
2025 年， Dario 薪酬约为 1800 万美元， Daniela 为 1640 万美元，均以股票和期权奖励为主。两⼈
及其他联合创始⼈已承诺，将其个⼈所持 Anthropic 股权的 80% ⽤于慈善事业。
Anthropic 董事会另有四名成员，将由公司设⽴的 " ⻓期利益信托 " 选举产⽣，现任受托⼈包括前
美联储主席本 · 伯南克和国家安全专家 Richard Fontaine 。
万亿美元 AI 军备竞赛： OpenAI 是最⼤对⼿

Anthropic 在 AI 领域⾯临来⾃多⽅的激烈竞争，其中最主要的对⼿是 OpenAI 。
两家公司在企业客⼾、顶尖⼈才及华盛顿政策影响⼒⽅⾯展开全⾯⻆⼒。据媒体报道， OpenAI
已于今年 6 ⽉秘密提交 IPO 申请，预计最迟于 2027 年初完成上市。
Anthropic 由⼀批因公司治理与 AI 安全理念分歧⽽离开 OpenAI 的研究⼈员于约五年前创⽴，并于
2023 年 3 ⽉发布⾸个⼤型语⾔模型，与 OpenAI 直接竞争。此外，该公司还与 SpaceX 旗下 xAI 、
Alphabet 旗下⾕歌及 Meta 在 AI 基础设施领域展开争夺。
Amazon 与⾕歌是 Anthropic 早期的两⼤战略合作伙伴，双⽅均向其注⼊数⼗亿美元投资，同时
为 Claude 模型的训练与部署提供云计算基础设施⽀持。
市场会对可靠、值得信赖且安全的 AI 系统予以回报。

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
AUGUST CPI PREVIEW
Softer August picture; risks skewed to the upside
14 September 2026
Key points
• August CPI to rise 0.43%mth boosting 
the annual pace to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr 
in July. On a seasonally adjusted basis, 
we expect a 0.7%mth increase. 
• August Trimmed Mean estimated to 
lift 0.23%mth, a step down from the 
0.4% average over the previous 3mths, 
holding the annual pace at 3.6%yr. 
• For Q3 we revised our CPI estimate 
to 1.3%qtr (from 1.1%qtr) while the 
Trimmed Mean is now expected to print 
0.9%qtr (vs. 0.8%qtr). A stronger-
than-expected July set a higher starting 
point which we expect to be partially 
unwound in August-September. We 
still see a moderation unfolding with 
Q4 Trimmed Mean now lower at 
0.7%qtr, and the CPI is now 0.5%qtr. 
• Further out, a higher interest rate 
profile, productivity gains from AI, 
and softer durable goods prices see 
inflation drift below mid-point of 
target, reaching 2.3%yr by end-2028.

BULLETIN
July saw more momentum than expected ...
The July CPI reversed May and June weakness with a larger-
than-expected 1.0% increase (see Inflation boosted by end 
of year price resetting ). This was stronger than Westpac’s 
expectation of 0.8% and market’s expectation of 0.9%. The 
annual pace eased to 3.5%yr from 3.8%yr due to a base effect 
of a stronger print a year earlier as electricity subsidies ended.
The Trimmed Mean (TM) lifted 0.5%mth (0.49%mth) and 
3.5%yr, on par with last July which was the strongest monthly 
read in the series’ short history. The result was stronger than 
both our and the market’s expectation of 0.4%mth and 3.5%yr. 
Meanwhile market services excluding volatiles increased 
0.9%mth/3.3%yr. This core measure of domestic inflation 
eased from 3.7%yr in June but is holding above the 3%yr pace.
Dwellings and rents were as expected, underpinning the 
TM gain. Upside surprises were mostly in durable goods 
(motor vehicles, clothing & footwear, household appliances, 
furniture & furnishing, audio visual & computing equipment) 
while household services (restaurants, domestic travel, 
other recreation activities, other services in respect of motor 
vehicles) made significant contributions. 
Looking forward to August it is clear that domestic inflationary 
pressures are elevated. The question for us is just how much of 
the July bump was due to larger-than-expected post financial 
year price adjustments following a larger-than-expected 
increase in minimum and Award wages, and how much is a 
sustained increase in underlying inflationary pressure. In this 
note we attempt to break this down. The bottom line is that, 
at least for now, we still expect core inflationary pressures to 
peak in the September quarter.
... while core inflation flattens in August
The August CPI is expected to rise 0.4% with the annual pace 
accelerating to 4.0%yr from 3.5%yr while our seasonally 
adjusted estimate lifts 0.7%mth/4.0%yr. Our estimate of the 
monthly TM is set to rise 0.2%mth lifting the annual pace  to 
3.6%yr. For market services excluding volatiles and holiday 
travel we have pencilled in a step down to 0.1%mth, this series 
lifted 0.8% in July possibly due to the larger-than-expected 
increase in minimum and Award wages, with the annual pace 
easing a touch (–0.1ppt) to 4.0%yr.
Jump in fuel offsets price falls elsewhere
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Neha Sharma, Economist

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The largest contributors to the monthly print come from
transport (0.51ppt) due to a solid gain in auto fuel (0.52ppt)
while both food and housing make a contribution (each
0.04ppt). For housing the gains from rents (0.02ppt) and

dwellings (0.03ppt) are partially offset by falling electricity
prices (–0.02ppt). Some offset is provided by recreation &
culture (–0.17ppt) with the most significant contribution from
holiday travel & accommodation (–0.20ppt). Also helping to
mute the August gains is falling household textiles (–0.02ppt).
Key details for August
Food & non-alcoholic beverages are expected to rise 0.2%mth
and 3.3%yr, largely due to solid gains for bread & cereal
products (0.4%), meat & seafoods (0.5%) and food products

n.e.c. (0.4%) partially offset by falling non-alcoholic beverages
(–0.6%) and a soft print for meals out & take away food (0.1%)
following the step up in July (0.9%).
As noted in our August Market Outlook , the impact of El
Niño on commodity prices is uneven, but the key areas of
exposure for Australia are in wheat and livestock. We are also
monitoring the spread of bird flu closely. There remains no
detections in commercial poultry to date.
Housing is expected to rise 0.2%mth in August, taking annual

pace to 5.7%yr. Component-wise:
• Rents are expected to rise 0.3%mth and 3.5%yr. We continue
to expect a modest firming in CPI rents into year-end given
the divergence with asking rents, although growth in asking
rents appears to have peaked and is gradually easing.
• New dwelling prices – covering the cost of the building
component only – are expected to rise 0.4%mth and 5.6%yr.
Disinflation remains gradual as capacity constraints persist
across the sector.

• In July the decline in electricity prices was less than expected
at –1.6%mth (we expected –4.4%mth due to new annual
market offers coming into effect). As such, we expect
some residual price resetting to show through in August
(–0.7%mth) but not by enough to prevent the annual pace
lifting from 6.1%yr to 12.4%yr.
Transport inflation is picking up with a 4.5% lift in August
taking the annual pace to 5.9%yr – it was 1.6%yr in July. The
return to full fuel excise, plus the CPI indexing of that excise,

occurred in August lifting auto fuel prices 16.6% in the month
boosting the annual pace to 15.4%yr from –0.4%yr in July.
Recreation & culture is expected to fall 1.4% in August.
Underscoring this is a 3.4%mth seasonal decline in holiday
travel, being partly offset by a 1.2%mth rise in audio, visual &
computing equipment & services.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Risks to August baseline
We view the risks around the August CPI outcome as skewed to
the upside. Price resets associated with the minimum and Award
wage decision should continue to flow through and may broaden
beyond award-reliant sectors. In mid-August, the Fair Work

Commission also approved a minimum wage for food delivery
drivers that sits above the national minimum wage, which could
place upward pressure on the takeaway foods component.
Cost pressures remain evident elsewhere. Trade materials
suppliers continued to announce above-average price increases
through August and September and while soft activity in the
established housing market may constrain the extent of pass-
through to new dwelling prices, these increases still present an

upside risk, including for maintenance, repairs and insurance.
In addition, there has also been anecdotal evidence of fuel
levies being reimposed as higher fuel costs continue to make
their way through the system. The recent up-tick in silver and
gold prices also presents upside risks to accessories which is
still seeing double digit annual price growth.
Was July noise or signal?
July’s stronger-than-expected CPI was accompanied by a
broadening in inflation pressures across the basket. Interpreting

this signal is not straightforward. The monthly CPI remains
volatile, the ABS is still building a sufficient history to identify
seasonal patterns across several components, and monthly TM
estimates remain subject to revision. While these revisions have
become smaller over time, the challenge remains distinguishing
temporary volatility – ‘noise’ – from a more persistent shift in
inflation dynamics – ‘signal’.
To assess whether the underlying inflation environment

may be changing, we have adapted the San Francisco Fed’s
Inflation Shock Momentum framework to Australian monthly
CPI data1. The approach identifies components experiencing
consecutive positive or negative inflation surprises and
aggregates these signals using expenditure weights.
In July, 14 of the 87 expenditure classes exhibited persistent
positive shocks, up from 8 in June – the highest since January
2024. Food was the most exposed category, accounting for

around one-third of positive shock momentum. On a 3mth moving
average basis, the expenditure-weighted share of positive shock
momentum increased to 11% of the basket in July from 7% in June,
while the negative momentum share declined to 19% from 21%.
As a result, the net balance (positive minus negative shocks)
lifted to its highest level since December 2025. The net balance
appears to be a relatively good lead indicator for annual
inflation in the quarterly TM, particularly at horizons of around

three quarters. While we do not think the latest readings signal
an imminent re-acceleration in trimmed mean inflation, they
suggest the disinflationary impulse may have started to fade.
1 Backcasted to 2018 for expenditure classes that did not have sufficient history. The original authors
use a 10-year rolling window for estimation, we use a 3-year rolling window.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Quarterly CPI sees more near-term pressure...
Relative to our August Market Outlook forecasts, we now
expect slightly stronger momentum in near-term inflation.
Recent data have pointed to a more resilient household sector
and larger-than-expected spillovers from the data centre

investment boom, both of which are expected to keep capacity
pressures in the economy a little more elevated. These
developments have lifted the probability of further policy
tightening sufficiently for Westpac to now expect the RBA to
raise the cash rate by 25bps in November (see here).
Westpac estimates headline CPI will rise 1.3%qtr in Q3, up
0.2ppts from our previous profile, leaving annual inflation
steady at 3.9%yr. Q4 headline CPI has been revised down

to 0.5%qtr (from 0.8%qtr previously), with annual inflation
easing to 3.7%yr. In year-ended terms, the remainder of our
profile through to end-2028 has been revised lower. We now
see headline inflation at 2.4%yr by end-2027, reaching 2.3%yr
by end-2028. All year-ended estimates are below the RBA’s
current published forecasts.
The seasonally adjusted CPI is estimated to increase 1.1%qtr
in Q3 and 0.6%qtr in Q4 highlighting that some of the strength

seen in both quarters is usual seasonality.
The upward revision to Q3 inflation is concentrated in alcohol
& tobacco (1.4%qtr, +0.4ppts), housing (1.5%qtr, +0.2ppts)
and transportation (2.6%qtr, +1.3ppts).
Key component revisions include rents (1.0%qtr, +0.1ppt),
utilities (1.8%qtr, +1.3ppts), automotive fuel (6.2%qtr,
+4.2ppts) and audio, visual & computing (4.1%qtr, +3.2ppts).
On this last component, the unprecedented pipeline of
global investment in data centres is driving strong demand

for semiconductors and computing equipment, indirectly
contributing to higher ongoing price pressures.
Partially offsetting these are downside revisions to food
(0.8%qtr, –0.2ppts) owing to a 1.5ppt downgrade to fruit &
vegetables. Holiday travel has also been revised 1.4ppts lower
to 1.6%qtr in Q3.

...but disinflationary impulse higher from 2027
We estimate the TM will rise 0.9%qtr in Q3, up from our
previous estimate of 0.8%qtr, with annual inflation easing to
3.5%yr. At two decimal places, our Q3 estimate is at 0.91%qtr,
up from 0.83%qtr. We have, however, lowered our Q4 trimmed
mean forecast to 0.7%qtr from 0.8%qtr previously, leaving our
end-2026 forecast unchanged at 3.3%yr.

We now expect a modestly faster pace of disinflation from
late-2027 onwards, with end-2027 at 2.7%yr (unchanged) and
end-2028 at 2.3%yr (–0.1ppt). This is supported by a higher
cash rate profile than previously through to the end of our
forecast horizon, alongside rising supply capacity on the back
of productivity gains from greater AI adoption and firm growth
in labour supply.
A key uncertainty remains the extent to which businesses
are able to pass through ongoing labour and non-labour cost

pressures. There is a risk that firms face greater resistance to
price increases if discretionary household spending softens
by more than expected following the unwinding of the
temporary fuel excise reduction (see here). In addition, some
domestic demand may be displaced by international travel
if holidays deferred earlier in the year due to Middle East-
related disruptions are taken in the second half of 2026. That
said, these headwinds may be at least partially offset by the

stronger starting point for household incomes, which has
proven more resilient than previously anticipated, and by higher
energy prices due to the ongoing conflict in the Middle East.

Document 234: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-01
Pages: 13
Status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

### Page 5

The dollar remains very well bid and the DXY dollar index is
close to the highs of the year. Yesterday's softer-than-
expected August US PCE inﬂation data barely put a dent in Fed
tightening expectations, perhaps because US activity data
continues to come in on the encouraging side. EUR/USD is
testing the lows of the year and looks vulnerable

Chris Turner, Frantisek Taborsky and Francesco Pesole
The dollar is testing the highs of the year and could go higher
USD: Activity data backs up the Fed's
hawkish position
The DXY dollar index is testing the highs of the year at 101.80.
Dollar debasement fears have been set aside for now as the cyclical
story takes centre stage. Here the US economy is performing
reasonably well and the market prices the need for the Fed to take
policy into restrictive territory. Even a seemingly more docile US PCE
inﬂation print for August, released yesterday, did not depress short-
dated US interest rates for long. For example, the one-month USD
OIS rate priced one year forward brieﬂy came oﬀ 5bp on the data

EUR: French debt sell-off must be a
concern
As yet, it is hard to identify that the sell-oﬀ in French debt is being
priced into the euro. EUR/USD has been driven to the lows of the
year by the hawkish reassessment of Fed policy rather than any
independent euro weakness. But the French OAT-German Bund
spread widening so quickly to +127bp is quite an alarming move –
potentially adding some risk premium into the euro as well as

constraining the ECB's tightening cycle.
That OAT-Bund spread will be in focus today now that the French
government has presented its plan to save EUR54bn and target a
budget deﬁcit at 5.0% of GDP next year. Have a look at a useful
piece on this subject here.
On the subject of the ECB, we hear from Joachim Nagel, Christine
Lagarde and Isabel Schnabel among others today. Expect them to
hold the hawkish line for the time being, but the chances of the ECB

'out-hawking' the Fed seem slim at this stage.
1.1300/1320 may feel like the bottom of the range in EUR/USD. But
if the OAT-Bund spread widens much further and US data stays
strong, we will have to prepare for a drop into the 1.11-12 area.
Chris Turner

CEE: Inﬂation clues and Romania’s
market test
Poland’s September inﬂation rose from 3.4% to 4.0% yesterday, in
line with expectations. The details oﬀer two key takeaways: we

estimate that core inﬂation eased from 3.3% to 3.0-3.1%, while
food prices stabilised after ﬁve months of decline. Softer core
inﬂation gives the National Bank of Poland room to wait,
supporting our economists’ view that the ﬁrst rate hike will come
only early next year. Still, November will be pivotal, as the central
bank’s new forecasts could open the ﬁrst window for a hike. The
stabilisation in food prices also oﬀers a signal for next week’s
inﬂation releases in the Czech Republic and Hungary, where food

prices, as in Poland, have kept inﬂation lower this year.
Today, we are watching PMI data across CEE, which have shown
relatively resilient industrial sentiment since the US-Iran conﬂict
began. In Romania, another attempt to form a government failed
yesterday, after which the president said he would select the next
candidate on 5 October. Despite no clear path to a new
government, markets remain stable. EUR/RON slipped from 5.280
towards 5.270 yesterday as part of a broader global relief rally. The

key test, however, will be S&P’s sovereign rating review on Friday.
Frantisek Taborsky

Document 306: Leading Index suggests momentum a touch below trend (2026-09-30)

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the likely
pace of economic activity relative to trend three to nine months
into the future, lifted to –0.09% in August from –0.17% in July.

The Leading Index shows momentum has continued to
improve and is only a touch below trend heading into late
2026 and early 2027. The June quarter national accounts
showed the Australian economy holding up better than
expected in the face of a global energy shock and higher
interest rates. Growth appears to be stabilising at a slow
pace rather than stalling with households resilient and
notable support coming from a strong ramp-up in data centre
investment. While a further rise in interest rates will take

some of the gloss off near term, Westpac now expects annual
growth to slow 1.5% by year-end, upgraded from our previous
forecast of 1% and broadly consistent with the improved
signal from the Leading Index.
The growth pulse is still clearly on the soft side. The Leading
Index growth rate has been below trend since the start of the
year, averaging –0.17%. This compares to the slightly above
trend reads recorded through most of 2025, which posted an

average read of +0.26%. However, the current below trend
run is noticeably milder than the weakness seen during the
protracted cost-of-living crisis that ran through most of 2022
to 2024. The average Leading Index growth read during this
period was –0.46% with lows closer to –1%.
The Leading Index growth rate is now roughly back where it
was six months ago, down only slightly from the –0.07% pace
back in February. That said, the detail points to significant

shifts in the growth mix and some fragility to the improved
signal. Component-wise the six months has seen slightly
bigger drags coming from labour markets, financial markets,
commodity prices and consumer sentiment, which have
taken 0.42ppts off the Index growth rate since February on
a combined basis. This has been balanced by a more positive
signal from dwelling approvals (adding +0.32ppts) and a
firming in US industrial production (+0.08ppts).

The mix does raise some questions about the extent to which
the recent improvement in momentum will be sustained.
Leading Index suggests momentum a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Rising fuel prices and concerns about further interest rate
increases look to be weighing on consumer sentiment again
with signs that the downturn in established housing markets
is also impacting. These effects could intensify near term and
may spread beyond the consumer to other index components.

The Reserve Bank Monetary Policy Board next meets on
September 28–29. While the latest Leading Index update
still shows the growth momentum is below trend, the soft
rather than weak pace suggests the economy’s relatively
resilient performance in the June quarter is likely to extend
through the second half of the year and early part of 2027.
This performance is adding to the Board’s unease that high
inflation remains ‘sticky’ and may track a slower path back to

the 2-3% target. We expect the RBA to raise rates again but,
on balance, hold off until another comprehensive quarterly
inflation update is released on October 28. That means the
September meeting is likely to be a ‘very hawkish hold’.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

People Bank of China (21 Sep)

We expect the PBOC to keep the 1Y LPR 
unchanged at 3.00%, maintaining a 
measured easing stance as resilient exports 
and industrial activity continue to support 
growth. Industrial production growth 
accelerated from 4.5% yoy in July to 5.2% in 
August, buoyed  by strong exports despite 
soft domestic demand. High -tech 
manufacturing expanded by 16.7% yoy , 
supported by the regional AI and electronics 
upcycle. Domestic momentum, however, 
remains uneven. Retail sales growth slowed 
from 0.6% yoy in July to 0.4% in August, 
weighed down by weaker purchases of big -
ticket items, including luxury goods, 
automobiles and furniture. Elevated 
precautionary savings and falling property 
prices continued to constrain household 
consumption. Investment conditions also 
deteriorated, with the fixed asset investment 
contraction widening from 6.7% yoy ytd  in 
July to 7.2% in August. State -owned and 
private investment fell by 3.6% and 10.1% yoy, 
respectively, while property investment 
declined by 19.9% yoy ytd as developers 
prioritised completions over new starts. 
Against this backdrop, policymakers are 
likely to maintain a measured policy stance, 
relying on targeted fiscal support to sustain 
growth rather than further rate cuts.

Bank Indonesia (23 Sep)

Bank Indonesia is expected to the 
benchmark rate unchanged at 5.75% this 
month. New Governor Destry is likely to 
highlight tighter global financial conditions, 
and geopolitical risks as key risks of the 
horizon, while the domestic economy 
continues to be r elatively resilient. After a 
considerable period of volatility in first half 
of the year, USDIDR has been rangebound 
this quarter, lowering the need for currency-
driven hikes. The incoming Finance Minister’s 
past statements also point to a continued 
preference for fiscal prudence rather than 
fiscal activism. We expect broad adherence 
to fiscal thresholds, a preference to  
reallocate expenditure than to boost 
borrowings, and build buffers in the math to 
absorb unexpected shocks, to add to macro 
stability. Inflation is off its back, but still 
within the BI’s target range. Against this 
backdrop and despite US Fed’s hike, we 
expect Indonesia’s policymakers to exhibit 
limited urgency to tighten policy at this 
juncture.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Hong Kong SAR 
Export growth is expected to accelerate 
from 50.7% yoy in July to 53.2% in August, in 
line with the pickup in China's export growth 
from 23.9% yoy to 25.0% over the same 
period. Trade momentum remains robust, 
supported by strong demand for AI -related 
electronics. Import growth is also expected 
to stay firm, reflecting sustained demand for 
intermediate and capital goods amid 
resilient export orders and gradually 
improving investment sentiment. 
On the inflation front, CPI is projected to 
edge up from 1.7% yoy in July to 1.8% in 
August, supported by improving 
consumption sentiment. The overall 
diffusion index strengthened during the 
month, driven by gains in the retail trade and 
restaurant sub -indices. Mainland visitor 
arrivals increased by 13.3% yoy in August on 
a daily average basis, providing further 
support to domestic consumption. Renewed 
Middle East conflict during the month will 
also provide upward pressure to headline 
CPI.

### Page 3

The Week Ahead September 18, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         3

Singapore 
We expect Singapore’s core and headline 
inflation to rise slightly to 2.1% yoy and 2.3% 
yoy, respectively, in August 2026, from 2.0% 
yoy and 2.2% yoy in July. The inflation 
readings likely reflected the continued 
lagged impact from higher global energy 
prices, such as a positive contribution from 
elevated electricity & gas price increases for 
a second consecutive month , and high 
transport inflation, while food inflation likely 
firmed. However, low albeit slightly rising 
accommodation cost increases and 
contained domestic services inflation should 
limit the overall increase in price pressures.

Document 386: Westpac Nowcast Q3 2026_ First Estimate (2026-10-01)

1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
WESTPAC NOWCAST Q3
2026: FIRST ESTIMATE
Growth picks up despite headwinds
25 September 2026
Key points
• Westpac-Now points to GDP growth
of around 0.6%qtr in Q3 2026 (with a
70% confidence interval of 0.29%qtr to
0.91%qtr) and 2.3%yr. This is a step up

from the 0.4%qtr and 2.1%yr recorded
in Q2 2026 (see here) and implies that
economic momentum has strengthened.
•  Our Monthly Activity Index has
stabilised after declining sharply since
November 2025 and is now showing
signs of improvement in July. This
provides further evidence the economy
is proving more resilient than initially
feared. Rather than deteriorating further,
activity appears to be weathering the
impacts of the Middle East conflict and

higher interest rates relatively well.
•  The RBA is unlikely to be comfortable with
this combination of improving conditions
and firmer economic momentum. Growth
at 2.3%yr would be close to our estimate
of ‘trend’, but it is above the RBA’s estimate
at a time when the Bank is trying to
engineer a period of below-trend growth
to ensure inflation returns to target.
• Overall, the results are consistent with
our view that the economy is being
supported by powerful business investment

tailwinds, centred on data centres
and renewable energy (see here). This
investment surge is largely unrelated to
the domestic business cycle or interest
rates and is increasingly offsetting
weakness elsewhere in the economy.

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
In November 2025, we introduced Westpac-Now (Westpac
Nowcast (Westpac-Now): The Upswing Accelerates |
Westpac IQ), a real time measure of current activity in the
Australian economy. Westpac-Now draws on more than
60 high frequency economic and financial variables, using

advanced statistical techniques to provide a timely pulse
check on economic momentum. It is the first Australian
model to incorporate internal banking data, enhancing its
predictive power.
This note provides the first estimate of GDP growth for the
September quarter 2026, using domestic and international
data released up to and including the August Labour Force
Survey (released 24 September 2026). Future updates will be
published monthly (see Appendix A for the release schedule).

Westpac Monthly Activity Index: Activity improves
as momentum builds
After weakening sharply through late 2025 and early 2026,
the Westpac Monthly Activity Index is beginning to recover.
While activity levels remain subdued, recent readings suggest
the economy has moved beyond the period of broad-based
deterioration seen earlier in the year. On a rolling three-month
basis, the Index has continued to improve, consistent with
improving momentum.
The weakness through late 2025 and early 2026 reflected

a broad-based deterioration across confidence, spending,
business activity and labour market indicators as households
and businesses adjusted to a renewed inflation challenge
followed by interest rates moving higher once again. More
recent data suggest these conditions have begun to stabilise
Economic momentum building
Pat Bustamante
Senior Economist
and, in some cases, improve. Business and consumer
confidence, household spending, and labour market indicators

have generally proven more resilient than expected given the
headwinds facing the economy.
This improvement is evident in our heatmap, where the
intensity and breadth of negative readings have moderated
noticeably. While pockets of softness remain, particularly
in interest-sensitive areas of the economy, fewer indicators
are showing deteriorating momentum and a growing
number are now contributing positively to activity. Business-
related indicators remain among the strongest contributors,

consistent with the substantial support currently being
provided by investment in data centres, renewable energy and
related infrastructure.
Our indicator heatmap decomposes changes in the Monthly
Activity Index into group-level contributions, allowing us to
assess which components are driving movements in activity.
For ease of interpretation, indicators are grouped into broad
categories (see the Appendix for further details). For example,
the labour market category includes both official ABS Labour

Force Survey outcomes and timelier survey-based measures
of labour demand and job advertisements.
Westpac-Now: Resilient growth amid rising headwinds
Recall that Westpac-Now is a refined indicator that combines
our Monthly Activity Index with advanced econometric
techniques to estimate GDP growth. As previously noted,
the fit is not expected to be exact. Measured GDP includes
components that are not well captured by high-frequency
activity indicators, such as imputed rents for owner-occupiers,

housing related ownership transfer costs, public and business
inventories, and depreciation.

### Page 3

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
To bridge monthly and quarterly data, we use a MIDAS
framework, allowing us to combine mixed-frequency inputs
and incorporate partial information for the current quarter
when producing GDP forecasts.
Based on current model estimates, Westpac-Now’s final

estimate for Q2 2026 points to GDP growth of 0.6%qtr
(with a 70% confidence interval of 0.29%qtr to 0.91%qtr)
and 2.3%yr, representing a step up from the 0.4%qtr and
2.1%yr outcomes recorded in Q2 2026.  This suggests that
economic momentum has strengthened, with the economy
continuing to show resilience.
The key question going forward is whether higher interest
rates, elevated energy prices and heightened geopolitical
uncertainty in the Middle East will begin to weigh more

heavily on demand, offsetting the substantial support
currently being provided by business investment. Our
forecasts suggest that growth will moderate, particularly
following the RBA Board’s September rate hike, with activity
slowing rather than stalling.
Looking ahead, model dynamics suggest growth will pick up
towards trend, at around 0.6%qtr in Q4 2026 and Q1 2027.
However, these projections remain much more tentative,
given their reliance on projected inputs rather than realised

data. In addition, heightened uncertainty, particularly around
the lingering impacts of the Middle East conflict, means risks
around these forward estimates are larger than usual.
September Quarter data: Momentum improving
Data released in the first month of the September quarter
indicates that the economy has picked up momentum relative
to the previous quarter, with our nowcast estimating Q3
growth at 0.6%qtr, in line with the 0.6%qtr “pure forecast”

based solely on data available up to June 2026.
This is similar to what occurred in Q2 2026. Following the
large downside surprise in April 2026, which coincided with
sharp falls in consumer and business confidence, growth
estimates stabilised over the remainder of the quarter. The
weakness in Q2 was concentrated in April, with subsequent
data pointing to stabilisation rather than further deterioration.
Early Q3 data point to a similar pattern, with activity showing

signs of resilience despite headwinds.
Note that monthly activity estimates are more uncertain than
normal when inflation is shifting. Faster price growth can cause
nominal indicators, such as consumer spending, to rise more
strongly even though ‘real’, inflation-adjusted spending may
be softening (and vice versa). The inclusion of ‘real’ indicators,
such as employment and banking transaction volumes, helps
mitigate some – but not all – of this uncertainty.

Bottom line:
Westpac-Now points to GDP growth of around 0.6%qtr in
Q3 2026 (2.3%yr), indicating that the economy remains more
resilient than previously feared. Strong business investment,
particularly in data centres and renewable energy, continues to
provide an important offset to soft demand in interest-sensitive
sectors of the economy, including the housing market.
Looking ahead, higher interest rates, elevated energy prices
and ongoing geopolitical uncertainty are expected to weigh

on activity, particularly following the RBA’s September rate
hike. However, our central case remains one of slower growth
rather than a material downturn.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Appendix A: Westpac-Now Release Schedule (2026)
Going forward, Westpac Now will be published on the Friday following each ABS Labour Force Survey (LFS) release, at 11:30am AEDT/AEST (Canberra time).
Each release incorporates the latest labour market data alongside other high frequency indicators and provides an updated nowcast for quarterly GDP growth.

As the quarter progresses, each update should be interpreted as a progressively more complete estimate of that quarter’s economic performance.
Release date (Friday) LFS reference month Quarter focus Interpretation
16 October 2026 September 2026 Q3 2026 – second estimate Refined Q3 signal as activity data accumulate
20 November 2026 October 2026 Q3 2026 – final estimate Near complete read on Q3 activity
18 December 2026 November 2026 Q4 2026 – first estimate Initial assessment of Q4 momentum

Document 391: “个人AI代理”凭什么收费？必须“省钱”而非“省时间”

Source PDF: all report pdf/“个人AI代理”凭什么收费？必须“省钱”而非“省时间”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-09-30

### Page 1

在 a16z 最新的播客访谈中， Assistant Bench 创始⼈ David Pawlan 认为，⼤众消费者真正
在意的不是效率提升，⽽是 " 免费的钱 "—— 如⾃动追讨机票降价退款、批量处理医疗报销、
优化⽔费账单。届时 AI 代理将重塑亚⻢逊、 Shopify 等平台的流量与⼴告逻辑。
摘要：
智能代理正从开发者⼯具迅速蔓延⾄普通消费者，成为继⼤语⾔模型问世、 AI 编程兴起之后，消
费级 AI 的第三次浪潮。这⼀赛道在过去数周内急剧升温，参与者已超过 120 家，但商业模式、⽤
⼾边界与⾏业格局均尚未成形。
9 ⽉ 29 ⽇知名⻛投机构 a16z 的播客中，合伙⼈ Anish Acharya 与其投资的 Assistant Benchmark 创
始⼈ David Pawlan 进⾏了⼀场深度对谈。
（左： a16z 合伙⼈ Anish Acharya ；右： Assistant Benchmark 创始⼈ David Pawlan ）
近期，从代码⽣成向个⼈助理演进，消费者 AI 智能体赛道迎来了真正的 “ 寒武纪⼤爆发 ” 。

Instinct 、 Muse 、 ChatGPT Voice 等产品的相继问世，彻底点燃了科技圈。
作为⾏业全景的观察者， David Pawlan 创⽴的 Assistant Benchmark 在上线短短 16 天内，便吸引
了超 10 万名独⽴访客，收到了⼏乎所有主流智能体创始⼈的联络。
当前，市场上已有 122 款不同类型的 Agent 跨越各个垂直领域，不仅展⽰了强⼤的技术潜⼒，也
暴露出市场在寻找可持续增⻓空间时的碰撞与焦虑。
David Pawlan 认为⼤众消费者并不在意效率提升 10% ，真正打动他们的是 " 免费的钱 " 。通过代
理⾃动追讨机票降价退款、批量处理 HSA 医疗报销、优化家庭⽤⽔账单。
与此同时 David Pawlan 强调，当 AI 代理开始代替⼈类与⼈类交互、代替⼈类与代理交互，亚⻢
逊、 Shopify 等平台的流量逻辑、⼴告模式乃⾄整个电商⽣态都将被迫重新定义。
赛道全景： 120 余个产品，竞争格局仍是 " ⼀⽚混战 "
据 David Pawlan 介绍，其运营的 Assistant Bench ⽬前已追踪到 122 款 AI 助理产品，涵盖⾯向消

费者的通⽤型助理和⾯向企业的⼯作流助理两⼤类别。

这条线，就会⽴即失去⽤⼾的所有信任。 ”

务的预订从 ' ⼈找⼈ ' 变成 ' 代理找⼈ ' ，再变成 ' 代理找代理 ' ，整个服务业的运作范式都将被重写。
这将是⼀个更加动态、更⾼频、也更有趣的次级经济体。

我们向所有助⼿发送相同的指令，⽐如「帮我订⼀张去芝加哥的机票」、「在我当前位置五个街区

⾥。发布后，反响出乎意料，到今天正好 16 天，⽹站访问量已经超过了 10 万，⼏乎所有相关创
始⼈都来联系了我。这件事本⾝也说明了⼀点：⼤家都在寻找答案，都想知道这个⾏业的⾛向。

动提交 HSA 报销申请；我⾃⼰⽤的⼀个场景是，每次订机票之后，如果票价下降，智能体会⾃
动联系航空公司申请差价积分。
还有⼀个我迄今为⽌最喜欢的⽤例：我⼀个朋友把 Grok Bot 接⼊了家⾥的灌溉系统，再连上天⽓
数据，让洒⽔器根据天⽓⾃动调节 —— ⽔费直接降了 50% 。
我觉得这类「隐形智能体」 —— 在后台默默执⾏省钱⼯作流、⽆需打扰你 —— 才是⾦融类真正的

爆发点，也是最符合消费者利益的⽅向。
Anish Acharya ：
对，⽽且这⾥⾯还有⼀个有趣的⼼理学逻辑 —— 没有⼈想听「省钱」这个词，⼤家想听的是「花
钱」和「⽩得的钱」。我觉得真正的钩⼦在于：这些场景会让⼈感觉像是在捡钱。翻出历史收据
提交 HSA 报销，是在捡钱；追回降价机票的差额，是在捡钱。这个钩⼦的吸引⼒⾮常强。
同时这类场景也有⼀个特点：低频但⾼价值。这让我对旅⾏和⾦融的判断趋同 —— 它们可能都会
成⻓为⼗亿美元级别的业务，但更可能是以垂直连接器的形式存在，⽽不是通⽤横向智能体。

为你服务。
还有专有数据的护城河 —— 你提到了专⻔针对纽约市的个⼈智能体。就像⼀个顶级礼宾会有⼀套
别⼈很难复制的城市品味，知道所有真正适合你的好去处。这种主客观兼备的专精化，我认为都
会发⽣。
David Pawlan ：
很有意思。我也注意到，就在我们的对话⾥，我们⼀会⼉说「 assistant 」，⼀会⼉说「 agent 」。
这个区别在 Twitter 上也⼀直有讨论 —— 这个⽣态的未来到底是「个⼈助⼿」还是「个⼈智能

### Page 13

体」？这两个词对你来说意味着什么？
Anish Acharya ：
这是个很好的问题。对我来说，「助⼿」是⼀种⽐「智能体」更低级的形态。智能体，你可以想
象它是⼀个社群⾥真正存在的成员。⽽助⼿，从定义上讲只能帮你完成你指派的任务，是被动执
⾏。智能体（ Agent ）有「⾃主性」（ Agency ），能主动让好的事情发⽣。
这跟企业侧的 AI 演进路径有点像 —— 从「实习⽣」开始，通过完成⼯作、不断提升能⼒，逐渐晋
升到更⾼层级的任务。在消费者⽣活⾥，⼀个助⼿怎么被「晋升」为智能体，被允许处理更精
细、更重要的事情？这是个值得深思的问题。
David Pawlan ：
我⾮常认同。我也在想这件事可能对社会层⾯的影响 —— 「智能体」这个词，会不会反⽽让我们
不够重视 AI 、不去把它拟⼈化？⽽「助⼿」会不会⼜加⼊了太多⼈情味，让社会感到不适？
这些词汇的演变⽅式，会因为环境不同⽽有很⼤差异 —— 你在旧⾦⼭，处于科技世界的中⼼；纽
约稍微滞后⼀点；⽽世界其他地⽅则会跟着慢慢赶上来。观察不同地⽅的⼈怎么理解和使⽤这些

词，会是⼀件很有意思的事情。
Anish Acharya ：
我倒不觉得这是「泡沫」，我更愿意把它理解为「靠近未来」 —— 我们就是站在时间轴稍靠前的
位置。
有趣的是，这件事可能反⽽能改善⼀些社交动态 —— 当你开始把智能体定性为「⾮⼈类」的时
候，它反⽽可以提供⼀种特殊的社交调节功能。有些事我对你说会很尴尬，或者群体⾥存在某种
让⼈不舒服的摩擦，⼀个设计良好的智能体也许能在不带情绪的前提下，帮助整理和化解这种局
⾯。
David Pawlan ：
就像是⼀个冲突的仲裁者。
Anish Acharya ：
某种程度上是。我不是在倡导⼀种更加被动攻击性的社交⽅式，但我确实觉得，智能体可以同时
成为社交互动的发起点和接收端。
David Pawlan ：
⽽且它会在很多⽅⾯产⽣显著的正向影响。你怎么看未来五年的图景 —— 这些智能体和助⼿，会
把我们带向何⽅？它们真的能让我们成为更快乐的⼈吗？
Anish Acharya ：
这才是我们真正应该追问的终极问题。我们谈了效率、⾦融、健康，很多都是世俗琐事。

我昨天在查资料，美国每年有 150 万⼈因醉驾被捕，这背后对应的⾏政负担是极其沉重的，⽽且
很多⼈根本负担不起律师。你想想，普通消费者承受着多⼤程度上不必要的官僚压⼒？所以第⼀
步，是怎么⽤智能体帮他们减轻这种压⼒ —— 财务上更优化，健康上更优化，在法律服务、家庭
理财等⽅⾯，享有过去只有富裕阶层才能负担的同等权利。

### Page 14

然后是更⼤的问题 —— 我妈妈常说，「知道⾃⼰想要什么，是最难的事」。当减轻了这些外部负担
之后，这些助⼿还能怎样帮助我们发现⾃⼰真正想要的是什么？我不认为答案只是设定⽬标那么
简单。很多⼈其实根本没有所谓的⽬标，他们只是在尝试找到⾃⼰是谁、怎么成为最真实的⾃
⼰。我们与世界的主观体验，加上这些机器的介⼊，可能会编织出⼀段很美的故事。
David Pawlan ：
完全同意。我能想象这样⼀个未来：我的孩⼦看到我们这代⼈⾛在街上低着头刷⼿机的⽼视频，
会困惑地问：「你们在⼲嘛？外⾯的世界那么美，你们却盯着屏幕？」希望智能体能帮我们把那
些枯燥的事情⾃动化，让我们作为⽗⺟真正陪伴孩⼦，作为年轻⼈真正享受和朋友在⼀起的时
光。不必再被那些「⽣活⾏政」拖着⾛。
Anish Acharya ：
对。这也是为什么我觉得编程智能体并不是在替代程序员的⼯作 —— 因为它在做的是⼈类程序员
从来不会做的事。我⾃⼰在造⼀个虚拟唱⽚店，可以⾛进去逛，我⼀会⼉发你链接，很酷。五
年、七年、⼗年前我绝对不会雇程序员为这个东西写代码，也不会花⼀年时间⾃⼰学。所以这些

事情从来就不是在和谁竞争，⽽是从⽆到有地创造出来的 —— 这不是成本压缩，⽽是可能性的扩
张。
David Pawlan ：
让⽣活更有趣了。
Anish Acharya ：
回到近期，我最近⼀直在思考⼀个问题，想听听你的看法：从 Mold Book 这类⼯具的经验来看，
「智能体原⽣系统」需要被怎样构建？ Instinct 做的智能体间⽹络，可能就是这个⽅向的⼀种尝
试。但感觉未来⼀定会出现专⻔为智能体之间交互⽽构建的基础设施，⽽这类东西今天根本不存
在。你怎么看？
David Pawlan ：
这会是⼀个全新的范式。你可以聊很久 —— 智能体有⾃⼰的邮件地址，有⾃⼰的电话号码。当预
订⾏为不再是「⼈找⼈」，也不再是「智能体代⼈去找⼈」，⽽是「智能体找智能体」，整个服务
业的底层逻辑都会被重新定义。
还有⽹络安全这⼀层 —— 互联⽹先来，⽹络安全后来；智能体先来，关于智能体的安全议题随之
⽽来。这⼜会催⽣出⼀个全新的⾏业。
但这就是资本主义的美妙之处：机会⽆处不在，每⼀个细分问题都在等待有⼈去解决。这⼀波浪

潮会带出⼀批初创公司，去建造这个新互联⽹、这个新数字世界的各个⻆落。
Anish Acharya ：
对，⽽且商业这块尤其值得期待。我⼀直把互联⽹看成三张⽹的叠加：商业⽹、应⽤⽹、内容
⽹。内容⽹已经深陷危机，应⽤⽹随着编程智能体的出现正在⼤幅上扬，商业⽹则⼀直做得不
错。
这周最典型的对⽐是： Shopify 选择拥抱 Muse ， Amazon 选择屏蔽 Muse 。这背后的逻辑其实很清
楚 —— 当流量进⼊这些平台的「前⻔」被重新定义，谁是赢家谁是输家就开始分野了。你怎么看
Amazon 和 Shopify 各⾃的选择？以及更宏观地看，谁会赢谁会输？

### Page 15

David Pawlan ：
Shopify 和 Amazon 是两种完全不同的商业模式。 Shopify 的核⼼使命是⺠主化商业， Amazon 的
利润来源是⼴告。
问题的核⼼就在这⾥：当你把⼈类的眼球从购物流程中移除，整个⼴告体系就失去了意义。
Amazon 的判断是：如果 Muse 和这些智能体开始代替⽤⼾完成购买，⽽ Amazon 拿不到⼀分⼴告
费，那整个利润模型就垮了。所以 Amazon 必须抵制 —— 它需要找到⼀套新的盈利范式，来适应
智能体代购的世界。
⽽对 Shopify 来说，智能体越普及，个体卖家触达买家的能⼒就越强，商业就越⺠主化 —— 这完
全契合 Shopify 的使命，当然选择拥抱。
接下来更有趣的是还没被触及的⾏业，⽐如餐厅预订。 Rezzy 最近开始封禁⽤浏览器⾃动化⼯具
抢座的账号，因为让智能体瞬间抢⾛所有位置，完全破坏了他们「⾯向⼈类的预订体验」的基
础。但当每个⼈都有了智能体，⼤家⼜回到了同⼀起跑线，餐厅反⽽可能重新获得权⼒ —— 按照
忠诚度、平均消费来决定把位置给谁，或者进⼊⼀个完全竞价的世界，谁出价⾼谁拿到位置。你

怎么看？
Anish Acharya ：
你说得很对。我觉得可以区分两类场景：
⼀类是供给稀缺型 —— ⽐如热⻔餐厅的位置。有了完美信息，餐厅最想要的当然是⾼单价、⾼频
次回头客，同时尽可能维持满座率。这是⼀个相当复杂的匹配计算，但专有供给的价值肯定会越
来越凸显，⽽不是依赖消费者端的摩擦来作为隐性防御。
另⼀类是需求稀缺型 —— ⽐如明天⻜旧⾦⼭的机票，供给充⾜但需求不⾜。这种场景下，智能体
可能催⽣⼀种「团购」模式：⼀批有意愿购买的买家聚集起来，供给侧的商家来竞相报价争取这
个集体需求 —— 谁能提供最好的体验，可能还是最低的价格。我们⼀直有「需求聚合」，但以如
此有利于消费者的⽅式来聚合供给，这在以前⼏乎没有发⽣过。
回到 Amazon 和 Shopify ，我觉得 Amazon 的判断是对的 —— 它不想被中间化，不仅会失去⼴告收
⼊，还会失去冲动消费。但我倒是有点好奇，智能体本⾝会不会也有「冲动消费」的倾向？毕竟
⼀个真正具备主动性的智能体，会尝试给⽤⼾带来惊喜和惊喜感 —— ⽽什么⽐⼀份意想不到的礼

物更令⼈愉悦呢？
我猜这些智能体并不会成为完美的理性消费者，它们会以全新的、⾮理性的⽅式作出选择。营
销、冲动消费、⼴告 —— 这些东西不会消失，只是需要转型。
David Pawlan ：
完全同意。这也引出了另⼀个问题：在这个智能体商业时代，推荐引擎究竟⻓什么样？⼤模型感
觉已经打破了传统的搜索质量。但如果你跟智能体说「帮我找双⽩袜⼦」，市场上有两万种⽩袜
⼦，它凭什么知道你喜欢哪种？
我觉得 Muse 在这⾥可能有先发优势 —— 扎克的整个逻辑就是对所有商业收税：有 Instagram 数
据，有 Facebook 数据，对⽤⼾的真实偏好理解更深。但推荐层在整个智能体商业体系⾥会⻓成
什么形态，还是很值得期待的问题。
Anish Acharya ：

### Page 16

对于⻓尾来说，这可能反⽽是件好事。就拿⽩袜⼦举例 —— 我当然可以去 Amazon 买，但也许我
的智能体会回来说：「我在 Etsy 上找到⼀位内布拉斯加的奶奶，她⼿⼯编织⽩袜⼦，要三周时
间，你要吗？」我想了想：⾏，听起来挺有意思的。
在没有任何利益冲突的情况下，智能体能审视所有选项，精准地为你量⾝定制 —— 这可能会带来
更多的惊喜感，反⽽⽐现有的推荐引擎更有意思。
David Pawlan ：
我太喜欢这个场景了，它能给⽇常⽆聊的购物体验加点料。
Anish Acharya ：
对于不爱逛街的⼈来说，购物确实挺⽆聊的。
更进⼀步想，可以更⼤胆⼀些：也许某天它不只是在 Etsy 上找到那位奶奶，⽽是直接给那位奶奶
的智能体发消息问：「你愿意接这个单⼦吗？」
这就是智能体之间协作真正有趣的地⽅ —— 每个智能体都代表着⾃⼰的⼈类在⾏动，有的提供产
品和服务，有的在创造收⼊。这样⼀个动态的、⾼频运转的次级经济体，也许⽐我们现在的世界
有意思得多。
David Pawlan ：
我脑⼦⾥出现了⼀个画⾯：内布拉斯加的⼀位奶奶，突然收到⼀条消息：「旧⾦⼭有位先⽣想要

你帮他织双⽩袜⼦，你愿意吗？」
Anish Acharya ：
哈哈，为什么不呢？当然愿意啊！
这件事还有⼀个让我很期待的地⽅：如果智能体真的在做正确的事，它选择产品的依据是真实的
产品质量，⽽不是谁的营销做得最好、谁的品牌最响。当然，如果⽤⼾明确说「我就要 Nike ，因
为我喜欢 Nike 」，智能体也会按照⽤⼾的明确意愿去执⾏ —— ⽽不是让消费者在各种营销暗⽰下
做出并不真正符合⾃⾝利益的选择。
David Pawlan ：
这打开了⼀个真正以消费者利益为中⼼的互联⽹的⼤⻔。⽽我觉得这个互联⽹，我们已经缺失很
久了。
Anish Acharya ：
完全同意。
顺便说，我们还没聊智能体的商业模式。在你追踪的 122 个产品⾥，你个⼈测试了 26 个。付费情
况是： 65 个收费，其中 35 个完全付费， 30 个是 Freemium （到上限后收费）；只有 13 个是免费
的。⽽ Muse 和 Instinct—— 也就是⽬前最强的两个 —— 都是免费的。还有 OpenAI 据说最近可能

也要发布什么东西。
那么，⻓尾初创公司怎么在这⾥活下来？为什么它们在巨头免费提供服务的情况下还在收费？

### Page 17

⻛险提⽰及免责条款

中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。

Anish Acharya ：
这会⾮常有挑战。据我们估算，如果真的要以很有野⼼的⽅式来做，每⽤⼾每天的成本⼤约是
20 美元，折合⼀年可能是数亿美元的开⽀，对初创公司来说是极⼤的压⼒。
有两个趋势值得押注：第⼀，浏览器⾃动化（ Browser Use ）的成本应该会像指数曲线⼀样下
降，变得越来越便宜。如果你今天融了资，赌的是六个⽉后这个成本会⼤幅下降，这是⼀场时间
上的博弈 —— 但我们确实已经看到模型 token 价格⼤幅压缩，所以这条路是有可能⾛通的，让更
多产品能做到免费。
但从另⼀个⻆度看，我并不喜欢「补贴⽤⼾」作为核⼼价值主张。我更期待看到的是⼀个每⽉收
1000 美元、但⽤⼾⼼⽢情愿为之付费的智能体 —— 就像⼈们愿意为各种⾮必需品每⽉花 1000 美

元⼀样，那是因为那个东西让他们⾜够兴奋。当⼀个智能体真正做到极致的市场适配，叠加上成
本不断下降的趋势，这就是我期待的路径。
David Pawlan ：
确实，拭⽬以待。
Anish Acharya ：
好，来⾃ Assistant Bench 的 David ，⼤家记得去看看这个⽹站。 David ，⾮常感谢你来。我觉得
我们可以每周都聊 —— 这个领域每天都在发⽣新事情。请⼤家关注他的 X 账号，也关注我，我们
⼀起在 X 社区⾥持续学习、保持同步。祝你和你的智能体⼀切顺利！
David Pawlan ：
谢谢你，这次聊得很开⼼。
Anish Acharya ：
好，再⻅！

Document 447: キャッシュレス化で店舗のコストは減るのか？－１円硬貨の増産と決済代行業者の破綻が映す「...

Source PDF: all report pdf/キャッシュレス化で店舗のコストは減るのか？－１円硬貨の増産と決済代行業者の破綻が映す「....pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

【要旨】 
キャッシュレス化が進めば店舗のコストは減っていくと思われがちだが、現実はそう単純ではない。
現金にクレジットカードやQR コード決済1などが積み重なるなかで、店舗は現金系とキャッシュレス
系の双方のコストを並行して負い続けている。本レポートは、消費者の利便性ではなく、各種決済を
受け入れる店舗側のコストに着目する。11 年ぶりの1 円硬貨の増産は現金の「減らないコスト」を、
決済代行業者の破綻は、事務負担の増加への対応に伴って「増えるコスト」やリスクを映し出す具体
例である。キャッシュレス化の進展は、決済に占める比率の上昇だけでなく、事務や資金繰りまで含
めた店舗の総コストの観点から捉え直す必要がある。

1 ―― キャッシュレス化は、店舗のコストを減らすのか

この数か月間、決済をめぐる対照的な２つの事象に接した。１つは、キャッシュレス化が進むなか
での、11 年ぶりとなる1 円硬貨の増産2である。 もう１つは、 加盟店に代わって複数のキャッシュレス
決済の導入・運用や、売上・入金管理などを支援する決済代行業者3の経営破綻4である。

1 「QR コード」は株式会社デンソーウェーブの登録商標である。 
2 独立行政法人造幣局が公式X アカウント（@JapanMint_IAA）に 2026 年 7 月 14 日、 「久しぶりに１円を量産していま
す！！！」との動画を投稿。各種報道では、財務省によると、セルフレジの増加が要因の１つとみられるとされる。 
3 決済代行業者の業務内容は多様であるが、本レポートでは加盟店とカード会社等の間に立ち、複数の決済手段の導入・運
用や、売上の集計・入金管理をまとめて請け負う事業者を広く指す。カード決済の国際的なネットワークを担う事業者か
ら、加盟店ごとの契約・精算を代行する事業者、カード会社等からの入金を待たずに加盟店へ売上代金を立て替え、早期に
支払う事業者など、その機能と規模は様々である。本レポートでは信用リスクとの関連で取り上げるが、これは「立替・早
期入金」を手掛ける業者に関わるものであり、決済ネットワークそのものの信頼性とは区別して論じる。 
4 大阪市中央区に本社を置く株式会社全東信が2026 年 7 月 6 日、大阪地方裁判所に自己破産を申請し、同日付で破産手続
開始決定を受けた。負債総額は約1259 億 2900 万円。同社はカード会社等からの入金に先立ち加盟店へ売上代金を立て替
える早期入金を主力としていた。各種報道では、破綻の要因として、コロナ禍における飲食店など加盟店の売上減少に伴う
収益減、長年にわたる粉飾決算の可能性などが挙げられている。 
2026-09-10 
研究員 
の眼 
キャッシュレス化 で店舗のコスト
は減るのか？ 
1 円硬貨の増産と決済代行業者の破綻が映す「減らないコスト」 
「増えるコスト」

総合政策研究部 上席研究員 新美 隆宏 
(03)3512-1803  t-niimi@nli-research.co.jp 
ニッセイ基礎研究所

### Page 2

2 ―― 店舗が負う決済コストの構造

店舗が負う決済コストを、キャッシュレス決済に伴うものと、現金決済に伴うものに分けて整理す
る。 
まず、キャッシュレス決済のコストである。設備面では、複数の決済手段に対応するマルチ決済端
末やセルフレジの導入 ・ 更新、 通信回線、 保守、POS システムとの連携などの固定費がかかる。 直接的
な費用としては、決済のたびに決済事業者へ支払う加盟店手数料が発生する。更に事務・オペレーシ
ョン面の負担も伴う。決済事業者ごとに締め日、入金日、手数料、明細の形式が異なるため、店舗は
POS 上の売上、各事業者の決済明細、銀行口座への入金の照合などを行わなければならない。返品や
取消が発生すれば、事業者やシステムに応じた処理が必要になり、従業員教育や顧客対応も伴う。 
一方、現金決済のコストも、現金授受を行う限りは継続的に必要となる。両替手数料などに加え、
釣り銭の準備、計数、銀行への入金、防犯といった業務である。ここで重要なのは、キャッシュレス
決済が普及して現金の取扱量が減っても、現金対応を維持するためのコストが同じ割合で減るとは限
らないことである。現金を授受する以上、取扱量が大幅に減っても、レジや金庫、締め作業や防犯と
いった基本的な対応は残ることになる。 
すなわち、店舗はキャッシュレスと現金の両決済のコストを、併せて負担することになる。キャッ
シュレス化は、現金コストを直ちに「ゼロ」にするものではなく、現金対応のコストが残る一方で、
新たなコストが加わることになる。とりわけ、端末や会計システムを統合し事務を自動化できる大手
企業に比べ、小規模店舗ほどこの二重の負担が重荷となりやすい。

3 ―― 「減らないコスト」と「増えるコスト」：２つの具体例

冒頭に挙げた２つの事象は、いずれも、キャッシュレス化が進んでも店舗の決済コストが単純には
減らないことを、異なる角度から映し出している。

＜1 円硬貨の増産 ～減らない現金インフラのコスト～＞ 
1 円硬貨の増産は、報道によると、セルフレジや自動釣り銭機の普及と関係している。省人化のた

### Page 3

＜決済代行業者の破綻 ～増える事務負担への対応と新たなリスク～＞ 
前章で見たように、決済手段が増えるほど、店舗の事務・資金管理の負担は膨らむ。この煩雑さを
軽減する手段として広がってきたのが、決済代行業者の利用である。決済代行業者を通じて複数の決
済手段をまとめて管理すれば、店舗は各事業者との個別対応が減り、入金の管理も一定程度集約でき
る。更に、通常の入金日より前に売上代金を受け取れる早期入金サービスは、店舗の資金繰りを支え
る。ただし、これにもコストが掛かり、注意すべき点もある。代行サービスの利用料や手数料が発生
し、早期入金などの付加サービスを利用すれば、その分の負担も上乗せされる。加えて、売上代金の
立替や早期入金を伴うサービスでは、店舗への入金が当該業者の信用力に依存することになる。今回
の経営破綻は、こうした立替・早期入金型のサービスに依存する場合、仲介事業者の信用力が加盟店
にとってのリスクとなり得ることを示した。ここで問題となるのは、増える事務負担への対応策とし
て選んだ手段が、新たなコストとリスクを伴うという、店舗にとっての選択の難しさである。

4 ―― キャッシュレス化を「総コスト」で評価する

以上のように、キャッシュレス化が進んでも、店舗の決済コストが減っていくとは限らない。現金
コストを負ったまま、キャッシュレス決済の手数料や設備費、事務負担、そしてその負担を軽減する
ための代行サービスの費用が積み重なる。1 円硬貨の増産は前者（減らない現金コスト）を、決済代
行業者の破綻は後者（増えるコストとその対応に伴うリスク）を、それぞれ具体的に示している。 
これを踏まえると、店舗にとっての課題は、決済手段を求められるままに増やすことではない。複
数の決済を1 台の端末で処理し、POS、 会計、 入金データを連携させるなど、 増えた決済手段を低コス
トで管理できる仕組みを整える必要がある。決済代行業者を利用する場合には、その手数料水準に加
え、立替・早期入金型では決済代行業者の財務の健全性も考慮する必要がある。 
そして、キャッシュレス対応の進展を測る際にも、決済に占める比率の上昇を求めるだけでは十分
ではない。現金管理、売上照合、従業員教育、障害対応、資金繰りまで含めた「店舗側の総コストの
変化」も併せて把握することにより、キャッシュレス化による店舗経営への影響が見えてくる。 
現金とキャッシュレスが併存する現在は、いわば両者のコストが重なり合う過渡期にある。レジ前
で消費者が享受する「選べる便利さ」の裏側で、この便利さを支える店舗側の負担構造にこそ目を向
ける必要がある。

また、本資料は情報提供が目的であり、記載の意見や予測は、いかなる契約の締結や解約を勧誘するものではありません。

沙特东西管道重启后，摩根⼤通最新数据显⽰中东⽯油出⼝ 10 ⽇均值已回升⾄战前⽔平的
89% ，原油流量更恢复⾄ 98%—— 但成品油出⼝仅 58% ，结构性缺⼝显著。与此同时，⾼
额运费正在吸引边际运⼒⼊场，霍尔⽊兹海峡 VLCC ⽇费率飙近 127 万美元历史⾼位，⼆⼿
船估值甚⾄超过新造船价。
摘要：
随着沙特东西管道恢复运营，中东⽯油出⼝动脉重新畅通，原油市场正在经历⼀场显著的供应
修复。
据追⻛交易台，摩根⼤通 9 ⽉ 29 ⽇发布的最新研究报告，中东地区⽯油出⼝总量的 10 ⽇均值已回
升⾄每⽇ 2050 万桶，相当于 2025 年战前⽔平的 89% ，与战前仅相差 11% 。摩根⼤通⼤宗商品研
究团队判断原油市场已基本实现正常化。这意味着尽管地区冲突仍在持续，供应端的实质性恢
复已在数据层⾯得到印证。
然⽽，复苏并不均衡。原油流量已反弹⾄每⽇ 1750 万桶，恢复⾄战前⽔平的 98% ，⽽成品油出
⼝仅为每⽇ 300 万桶，仅相当于战前的 58% 。这意味着原油市场虽已⼤体正常化，精炼产品供应

的缺⼝依然显著，相关市场仍⾯临结构性压⼒。
原油与成品油：复苏分化明显
报告数据显⽰，此轮中东⽯油出⼝的回升呈现出明显的内部分化格局。
原油⽅⾯，每⽇ 1750 万桶的流量已接近战前正常⽔平（约 1800 万桶），恢复⽐例⾼达 98% ，表
明上游⽣产和原油运输环节已基本恢复正常运转。
成品油⽅⾯，情况则截然不同。⽬前每⽇ 300 万桶的出⼝量，仅为战前正常⽔平（约 520 万桶）
的 58% ，缺⼝超过 40% 。这意味着中东地区的炼化产能或出⼝基础设施仍受到较⼤制约，柴
油、航空燃油、汽油等精炼产品的供应紧张局⾯短期内难以完全消除。
